  Queen at f5: diagonals are’f5-g4-h3’ and ’f5-e4-d3-c2-b1’.
  Wait, f5 to g3 is not a queen move.
  Wait, what about the Rook at b2? b2 to g2 is a move, but b2 to g3 is not.
  What about the Rook at g8? g8 to g3 is a move!
  Yes! The Rook at g8 controls the entire g-file.
  So the White King cannot move to g5, g4, or g3.
- h3: Empty. If K moves to h3, does anything attack h3?
  Queen at f5 attacks h3 (f5-g4-h3)!
  So K cannot move to h3.

So, the King is completely immobilized!
It cannot move to h5 (Queen), g5 (Queen), g4 (Pawn h5 and Rook g8), g3 (Rook g8), or h3 (Queen).
Thus, the only available moves are the pawn moves!

Now, let's evaluate the pawn moves:
- h2h3
- g2g3
- g2g4

White is in a desperate position.
Black has a Queen, two Rooks, and a Bishop.
White has a King and three pawns.
White needs to survive.
The pawn moves:
- g2g4: This blocks the Rook at g8 and the pawn at h5.
  Wait, g2g4:
  Now the King at h4 can move?
  If pawn is at g4, the King cannot move to g4.
  But the rook at g8 is now blocked by the pawn at g4.
  Does that help?
  The King still cannot move to g5 (Queen), g3 (Rook g8 is blocked by g4, but g3 is’below’ g4... wait, the Rook is at g8, so it attacks g7, g6, g5, g4, g3, g2, g1. If white plays g2g4, the rook at g8 now attacks g7, g6, g5, g4, but is blocked from g3, g2, g1).
  So, if White plays g2g4, the King can now move to g3!
  Let's check other moves.
- g2g3:
  The Rook at g8 is still attacking g3. So the King still cannot move to g3.
- h2h3:
  Doesn't help the King's mobility.

So g2g4 seems like a strong move because it potentially frees the King and blocks the Rook.
Wait, if White plays g2g4, the pawn at g4 is attacked by the Rook at g8 and the pawn at h5.
Black can capture g4.
If Black captures g4 with the rook (Rxg4), the Rook is now at g4.
If Black captures g4 with the pawn (hxg4), the pawn is now at g4.

Is there any other better move?
Let's look at the moves again: h2h3, g2g3, g2g4.
If we play g2g4, we create some space.
If we play h2h3, we just move a pawn.
If we play g2g3, we move a pawn.

Let's consider the position again.
White: K(h4), P(f4, g2, h2)
Black: K(d6), Q(f5), R(b2, g8), B(f8), p(a7, f7, f6, h5)
The most active black piece is the Queen at f5 and the Rook at b2 (which is very deep in white's territory).
The Rook at b2 is attacking g2 and h2.
If White plays g2g4, the Rook at b2 still attacks h2.
If White plays g2g3, the Rook at b2 still attacks h2.
If White plays h2h3, the Rook at b2 now attacks g2.

Wait, if g2g4 is played, the Pawn at g4 is attacked by h5 and g8.
If Black plays hxg4, then h4 is no longer attacked by the pawn at h5.
But h4 is already not attacked by h5.
Wait, the Rook at g8 was attacking the g-file.
By playing g2g4, White blocks the g-file for the Rook at g8.

Let's double check the legal moves once more.
h2h3, g2g3, g2g4.
Is there any reason to prefer g2g4 over the others?
g2g4 helps immobilize the Rook at g8's influence on g3.
Let's try g2g4.
